- An M domain (methionine-rich domain) that binds signal peptides
- A C-terminal domain

The sequence shows:

2. The protein is approximately 400 amino acids long, consistent with Ffh/SRP54

The question states that &quot;A nucleotide-binding feature by itself does not identify a protein&#x27;s role&quot; - this is hinting that while we see a GTPase motif, we need to look at the broader context.

Given the overall architecture (GTPase domain + additional domains), the size (~400 aa), and the characteristic features, this protein is most likely Ffh (SRP54 homolog in bacteria), which is a component of the SRP-dependent protein-targeting system that works with the FtsY receptor.

Let me verify against the other options:
- Option B (Tat system): Tat receptors are typically smaller and have different architectures. Tat doesn&#x27;t use GTPases in the same way.
- Option C (Flagellar system): Flagellar export uses ATPases (FlhI/FlhB) but these have different domain architectures - typically AAA+ ATPases with different motifs.
- Option D (Proteasomal system): Proteasomal ATPases are AAA+ family ATPases with different domain organizations.

The SRP system uses Ffh (bacterial SRP54) which is a GTPase that pairs with FtsY (the SRP receptor). The GTPase domain architecture with Walker A motif and the overall size and domain organization are consistent with Ffh/SRP54.

Therefore, the answer is A.
